Table 7-10 (`dc21285.txt:6821`) and the IRQEnable prose (`:6911`) make it a documented READ whose value can only be changed via EnableSet/EnableClear -- and **the model already computes `d->irq_enable` and then discards it by dying**.  So: delete the kill, KEEP the value, no latch and no message, because there is no event to announce.  This is the FOURTH option-set member, and a bounded-announce here would ship a wrong record in product code.  (2) **DECLINE + BOUNDED ANNOUNCE, five sites, one class**: the invalid-DIRECTION interrupt accesses -- WRITE to IRQStatus `+0x180` and FIQStatus `+0x280` (read-only, `:6886`), WRITE to IRQRawStatus `+0x184` and FIQRawStatus `+0x284` (read-only, `:6901`), and READ from ENABLE CLEAR `+0x18c` (write-only).  **`+0x18c` carries an extra obligation the others do not: the decline must NOT expose the currently assigned `d->irq_enable`**, which is the value a naive 'just return the field' fix would leak.  (3) **DECLINE + an ACCURATE announce, two sites, a DIFFERENT rationale**: the SDRAM Base Address Mask `+0x100` (`:5844`) and Offset `+0x104` (`:5897`) reads.  These registers are R/W and legal -- **the model simply does not implement them** -- so the message must say `unimplemented read`, NOT `invalid access`.  Getting that wording wrong is the same wrong-record defect as (1), one step milder.  *** THE OPERATIONAL TRAP, AND IT IS THE SHARPEST THING IN THIS FINDING: `gate_arm.sh` REQUIRES THE `+0x188` KILL TO KEEP HAPPENING. ***  Its row is `check \"footbridge: the one-instruction kill arm still dies\" ... '^K1 .*ok$' ... 1`, and `K1` is precisely the ENABLE-SET read the ACCEPT round would fix.  **So the ACCEPT round turns a GREEN GATE RED by succeeding**, and the fix is to REPOINT K1 at `+0x18c` in the same commit.  A round that does not know this reads the red as a regression in its own work.  Verified by opening the gate.  **AND THE PROBE'S FAILABILITY LEG RESTS ON A PRODUCT DEFECT**, which is fine until it is the LAST one: `footbridge_sites_probe.py` reaches exactly one fatal site (K1) and uses its death as the proof the guest code got that far.  Once the final live kill is repaired there is no product defect left to point at, and the leg must become a MUTATION/failability leg instead.  That is a real cost of finishing this cluster and it should be planned, not discovered.  DETECTOR DEBT the design already owes: seed `irq_enable` with a NONZERO write to `+0x188` before reading it, so the ACCEPT row cannot pass on the reset zero; a row per remaining live site checking survival, accurate bounded reporting AND no state mutation; and for `+0x18c`, that the invalid read neither clears `irq_enable` nor returns it.  **A PER-SITE LATCH BITMASK, not one shared latch** -- otherwise the first access kind to fire suppresses the first useful report for every other, which is `#490`'s measured `M3shared` shape.  *Provenance and its limit: the datasheet citations are `dc21285.txt` line anchors from a seat that read the file. Run A recorded that obligation but kept the site in the same group.  **Run B's split is the better one on the round-scope rule's own text** -- a site needing a state-suppression the others do not is not covered by the same fix design -- but it is a judgement call and it is recorded as one.  *** SPLIT 2, AND IT PROPOSES A FIFTH OPTION-SET MEMBER: `IMPLEMENT`. ***  For the two SDRAM Base Address registers (`+0x100` mask, `+0x104` offset) Run A said decline + an ACCURATE announce (*'unimplemented read'*, not *'invalid access'*).  Run B said flatly **'none of the four offered options fits'** and proposed implementing them: reset-zero state with masked write/readback.  **The disagreement is real because these registers are LEGAL AND READ/WRITE.**  The existing four members all presume the access is either unserviceable (delete / decline / decline+announce) or already correctly computed (ACCEPT).  A legal R/W register the model simply never implemented is neither: declining it announces truthfully and still returns a wrong answer to a guest that wrote then read back.  **THE ADDITION LICENCE PUTS THE BURDEN ON `IMPLEMENT`, and that is the right way round:** additions to product code here are licensed by a MEASURED DELTA or a TRANSFERRED WARRANT, never by 'it cannot hurt'.  Implementing two registers is an addition, so it owes one of those -- a guest that actually reads them back, or an in-tree precedent for modelling an SDRAM sizing register.  **Until one exists, decline+accurate-announce is the licensed choice and IMPLEMENT is the filed alternative.**  A round taking these two sites must answer this explicitly rather than inheriting whichever note it reads first.  *Recorded now because the four-member option set is carrier doctrine, and a proposed FIFTH member is a rule question -- which this project's own splitting rule says goes to the adjudicating seat at the next round boundary rather than being settled in passing.*  *** THE K1 GATE DEPENDENCY IS VERIFIED INDEPENDENTLY, NOT TAKEN ON THE DESIGN'S WORD. and left GXEMUL-SEC untouched (verified by `git status --porcelain`: no src file modified). *** THE FLOOD IS CONFIRMED AND IS WORSE THAN THE TITLE SAYS: *** 20 stores produce 20 `fatal()` lines WITH and WITHOUT `-q` (0 `debug()` lines either way), and the OVER-READ site at 500 iterations produced 2000 lines / 158,449 bytes of host log (`regress/artifacts/wdcflood/witness_20260825/n3.out`, preserved off the volatile session scratchpad this row used to cite). *** CORRECTED IN PLACE 2026-08-26: this sentence said \"the read path\", which points a reader at the non-8-bit READ guard. `n3.out` is 2000x \"reading too much\" -- the RING OVER-READ. Same numbers, wrong site named. The correction had already landed in the round block, the source comment and the gate comment while this field kept the false wording -- which is the corrections-land-in-CHANGELOG-only failure this ledger records having made twice before, now three times. It was found by a mechanical sweep for stale claims across all five records, not by reading. *** *** BUT `:583`, THE SITE THIS ROW NAMES, IS THE MORE GATED OF TWO FLOODS. *** It requires an attached IDE image (`:461-468` early-returns with a silenceable `debug()` otherwise) and yields ONE line per store. The unnamed sibling `wdc_get_inbuf` over-read at `:160` requires NOTHING -- landisk adds wdc unconditionally at machine_landisk.c:90 -- is on the READ path, and yields FOUR lines per single guest instruction (measured: mov.b/mov.w/mov.l x20 give 20/40/80). SIBLING CENSUS: ten `fatal()` in dev_wdc.c, FOUR latched by #337, SIX UNLATCHED -- :145, :160, :583, :607, :704, :1025. **Fixing :583 alone leaves the strictly worse :160 floodable at 4x with no precondition**, which is the project's recorded 'grep for its siblings' failure. SCOPE RULING SOUGHT: the round is the FILE, not the line -- six sites, one design, one detector. *** WHY #337 MISSED IT, AND IT IS EXPLICABLE RATHER THAN AN OVERSIGHT: *** that round's scope was \"each replaced `exit(1)`\", and `:583` was always a `fatal()`, never an `exit(1)`, so it fell outside the enumeration. *** THE OPTION-SET PREMISE WAS REFUTED, AND IT WAS MINE. *** The three-membered option set rule describes member 3 as \"announces the first N occurrences and then goes quiet\". That describes `memory_rw.c:495-523` -- which is the tree's ONLY counter-style bound, is DOUBLE-GATED so the counter never increments at default verbosity (`&&` short-circuits), is GLOBAL not per-instance, and prints no give-up notice. The house idiom is different and mature: **N=1 PER-INSTANCE LATCH plus an explicit \"(further occurrences suppressed)\" in the message text**, at roughly 34 sites across 11 device files. `dev_wdc.c` itself holds FOUR (`warned_badlen`, `warned_atapi_err`, `warned_atapi_dataout`, struct at :75-77) and is the tree's REFERENCE implementation -- dev_dreamcast_gdrom.c:147 says its own latch is \"Modelled on dev_wdc.c's warned_badlen (#337)\". *** AND `fatal()` SHOULD BE KEPT, ON A RECORDED HOUSE WARRANT: *** dev_asc.c:250-257 states that under `-q` verbosity settles at VERBOSITY_ERROR, so a WARNING-level debugmsg would be INVISIBLE to the boot-log hygiene grep and an ERROR-level one would trip #261's break-on-ERROR. The chronology runs one way -- #245 chose verbosity-gating; #265/#269, #337, #460, #461 all chose latch + fatal(). TWO HAZARDS FOR THE DIFF, both from the seat: (1) SKETCH RULE -- DEVICE_ACCESS(wdc) has a common tail at :1019-1030 (`ret:` -> memory_writemax64 -> return 1), so any decline at the ACCESS level must `goto ret`; `wdc_command()` itself is void and called at :1003, so returning inside IT is safe. (2) CLAUSE (d) -- the guard would read the new `warned_*` field while the body reads `d->cur_command`, so the detector owes a state where a SECOND, DIFFERENT unimplemented command arrives after the latch has set; the 0x5a-vs-0xaa control is the seed for that row. OPEN QUESTION THE SEAT DECLINED TO SETTLE, recorded as open rather than guessed: #337 notes its two warned_badlen sites SHARE one flag, while #460's gdrom comment records a MEASURED warrant that per-site bits beat a shared bit when two sites run in one session. For pure flood control a shared latch is strictly better; the tradeoff is information loss, and which matters here is unmeasured. PRIVILEGE, stated exactly: no device-layer check exists and memory_rw dispatches on physical address, so \"unprivileged\" overstates it -- the exact claim is \"any code that can reach the I/O window, with no emulator-side gate\".  *** OWNER SCOPE DECISION 2026-08-25: THE ROUND IS THE WHOLE FILE -- ALL SIX UNLATCHED SITES, ONE DESIGN, ONE DETECTOR. *** Asked to choose between the filed single site, the two measured floods, and the file, the owner took the file. So the round covers :145, :160, :583, :607, :704 and :1025 using dev_wdc.c's own #337 idiom (per-instance latch + \"(further occurrences suppressed)\"), keeping `fatal()` on the dev_asc.c:250-257 warrant and keeping the existing `d->error |= WDCE_ABRT`. The reopening rule permits it: same device, same semantic class, one fix design, one detector, no new oracle. **Only :160 and :583 carry a rung-3 witness today; the other four are joined on the SAME-CLASS clause and each should get a one-store witness in pass 1, per #337's measured partial-file miss.**  *** PASS 1 (panel_20260825_195526): agy SETTLED Q1 WITH AN ARGUMENT NOBODY HAD MADE, AND IT DISSOLVES THE EVIDENCE CONFLICT RATHER THAN PICKING A SIDE. *** The brief posed shared-vs-per-site latching as #337 (shares `warned_badlen` across two sites) versus #460 (measured warrant for per-site bits). **Neither is wrong: #337's two sites are the two arms of ONE ENDIANNESS BRANCH.** VERIFIED BY READING: dev_wdc.c:709 is `if (cpu->byte_order == EMUL_LITTLE_ENDIAN) {` containing :731, and :739 is its `} else {` containing :761. A CPU does not flip endianness mid-session, so those two are MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE AT RUNTIME and sharing a flag loses exactly zero information. #460's warrant governs sites that can BOTH fire, which is all six here. **DESIGN SETTLED: SIX separate per-instance latches** (or one bitfield with six masks), never shared across read/write or overrun/over-read pairs. *** Q2 SETTLED: NO SUPPRESSION NOTICE. *** Census verified independently by agy against all of `src/devices/`: only dev_asc.c (3 sites) and dev_vga.c (1) carry it, and dev_wdc.c's own four #337 sites do not. Adding it to six new sites would make the file internally inconsistent; editing the four existing ones is unjustified churn into working code. *** Q3 ANSWERED, AND IT CARRIES AN IMPLEMENTATION TRAP THE NAIVE FIX WALKS INTO. *** `:160` is a GUEST error and is safe to latch -- real hardware returns open bus on a read past the transfer, and the emulator returns `(uint64_t) -1`. **BUT the `return (uint64_t) -1;` MUST STAY OUTSIDE the latch guard.** Nesting it inside `if (!d->warned_...)` makes the SECOND over-read fall through to `d->inbuf_tail = (d->inbuf_tail + 1) % WDC_INBUF_SIZE;` and CORRUPT THE RING POINTER -- the fix would introduce a defect worse than the flood it removes. Same shape at `:1025`, inside the common tail: guard the `fatal()` only; `data[0] = odata;` and `return 1;` run regardless. *** Q5: agy CAUGHT AN INTERNAL CONTRADICTION IN MY OWN BRIEF. *** The proposed design specifies scalar `warned_*` fields while Q5 demanded a detector catch a per-device-versus-per-opcode mutant -- which would require a 256-bit bitmap (`uint32_t warned_unimpl_cmd[8]`). Resolution: the scalar per-site latch is correct (it is what stops a guest cycling 0x00..0xff for 256 lines), and **the TRUE clause-(d) mutant is a different one: scope `d->error |= WDCE_ABRT` INSIDE the latch guard at :583.** Then the second unimplemented command silently SUCCEEDS. A detector that counts only host log lines passes it clean. That is a better (d) mutant than the one the brief proposed and it is the round's headline detector obligation. TWO FURTHER EVASIONS from the same seat: nesting the `return -1` at :160 (above), and using `static int warned` instead of a per-instance field, which silences a secondary IDE controller at 0x170 because the primary at 0x1f0 already warned. *** Q7 WITNESS COSTS, one of which is a genuine obstacle: *** `:607` = a 16-bit write to wd_seccnt; `:1025` = a 16-bit read from wd_status; `:704` = a store to wd_data with no active command -- all cheap. **`:145` is NOT cheap: WDC_MAX_SECTORS is 512 and WDC_INBUF_SIZE is `512*(512+1)` = 262,656 bytes (verified by reading dev_wdc.c:47-48), so filling the ring by guest stores costs ~65,664 32-bit stores.** The disk-read fill path at :343 moves 512 bytes per sector and would be far cheaper, but it needs an attached image -- i.e. it needs `landiskdisk` first. ALL THREE of agy's checkable claims were VERIFIED against source rather than credited: the endianness branch, the buffer arithmetic, and that wdc_get_inbuf() is called once per BYTE inside 16/32-bit transfers (:616, :620, :622, :623 little-endian; :627, :629, :630 big-endian), which is exactly the measured 4 lines per 32-bit guest load.  *** FULL PASS-1 PANEL COMPLETE (panel_20260825_195526): 8 SEATS ANSWERED. *** codex 501 KB, grok 19.5 KB, minimax 13.6 KB, agy 13.2 KB, kimi-k3:cloud 10.8 KB (the SUBSTITUTE transport, fired by hand -- the CLI is 403-walled), glm 4.4 KB, deepseek 4.1 KB. The kimi CLI is the only seat failure and it is a transport wall, not a seat outage. *** Q2 IS UNANIMOUS ACROSS EVERY SEAT THAT ANSWERED IT: NO SUPPRESSION NOTICE. *** *** Q1 SPLIT 3-1-2, AND THE SPLIT IS DECIDED BY EVIDENCE RATHER THAN COUNT. *** SIX separate latches: agy, grok, kimi. FIVE plus a per-opcode set for :583: deepseek. FOUR (pairing): glm, minimax. **The two pairing seats both cite #337's shared `warned_badlen` as the licence -- and that precedent is measurably not a licence.** agy and grok independently derived that #337's two sites are the two arms of ONE ENDIANNESS BRANCH (:709 `if (cpu->byte_order == EMUL_LITTLE_ENDIAN)`, :739 its `} else {`), so they are mutually exclusive at runtime and sharing loses nothing. grok put it exactly: *\"two endian copies of one check, not a licence to pair different events.\"* kimi added an independent refutation from THIS ROUND'S OWN WITNESS: the :160 over-read fired with NO DISK ATTACHED, so no overrun could have occurred -- **the two ring halves are causally independent, which is precisely what the pairing assumes they are not.** DESIGN SETTLED: six per-instance flags, wrap ONLY the `fatal()`, leave every guest-visible side effect outside the guard. *** Q3: FOUR SEATS INDEPENDENTLY LOCATE THE REAL DEFECT AT :145, NOT :160 -- see `wdcringambig`, filed. *** glm: *\":145 is an emulator error, do NOT latch without fixing the ring overrun.\"* minimax: *\":145 IS a real defect that the latch does not fix -- it corrupts the ring and continues, so a TODO is owed there.\"* kimi located it first and most precisely. grok: *\":145 is the unfixed pckbc/asc wrap, not a twin of the measured :160 flood.\"* Only agy said :160 was the site to worry about, and it was answering the question as the brief framed it. *** Q5 WAS CONFUSED IN THE BRIEF AND THREE SEATS SAID SO. *** grok: *\"Q5's per-opcode (d) mutant contradicts the per-instance design the brief actually proposes -- that mutant is a spec change, not a floor test.\"* minimax and agy said the same. deepseek dissents usefully in the other direction: :583's diagnostic VALUE is the opcode, so a single flag hides distinct guest actions and a 256-bit set would preserve it. **Disposition: per-instance scalar for the fix (it is what stops a guest cycling 0x00..0xff for 256 lines); deepseek's per-opcode proposal is a SPEC IMPROVEMENT and is filed, not folded in.** The real (d) mutants, from three seats: scope `d->error |= WDCE_ABRT` INSIDE the latch at :583 (agy -- the second unknown command then silently SUCCEEDS); nest the `return (uint64_t) -1;` inside the guard at :160 (agy -- corrupts inbuf_tail); and **reuse a GUEST-RESETTABLE field such as `d->seccnt` as the latch (glm)** -- the sharpest of the three, because the guard then reads a field the guest controls. *** THE :145 WITNESS IS NOT A ONE-STORE WITNESS, AND codex SUPPLIED THE TWO HONEST ROUTES: *** prime `head = WDC_INBUF_SIZE - 1, tail = 0` in the detector and call the producer once; or, for a real guest-path measurement, attach an image and issue **513 undrained IDENTIFY commands** -- each adds 512 bytes through `wdc_addtoinbuf`, and the final byte of the 513th reaches the 262,656-byte wrap. The second route needs `landiskdisk`. *** AND A DETECTOR RULE FROM codex THAT APPLIES TO EVERY ROW: each site row must FIRE THE SITE TWICE. \"A one-off first warning proves announcement, not suppression.\" *** A row that triggers a site once cannot distinguish a working latch from no latch at all -- which is this project's vacuity floor, stated for the latch class specifically.  *** A WRONG RECORD IN THIS ROW, WRITTEN BY ME AND CAUGHT BY THE ADJUDICATOR. CORRECTED 2026-08-25. *** Earlier in this note minimax is attributed the position that :145 is a real defect the latch does not fix, and is COUNTED AMONG SEATS SAYING DO-NOT-LATCH. **That count is FALSE.** minimax's Q3 says verbatim: **\"So: latch both.\"** Its position is latch-and-mark, which is the disposition this round adopted -- it was recorded as dissenting from a ruling it agreed with. *** THE DON'T-LATCH CAMP IS glm ALONE *** (\"`:145` is an emulator error, do NOT latch without fixing the ring overrun\"), which turns a 2-of-8 reading into 1-of-8. **AND glm's OWN FALLBACK SILENCES HARDER THAN THE LATCH IT OBJECTS TO:** it proposes \"downgrade it to `debug()`\", and `debug()` has the early-out at `core/debugmsg.c:375` -- free-running at `verbose == 0` gives `v < 0`, so it vanishes ENTIRELY in production, while a latched `fatal()` still announces the first occurrence and cannot be silenced by `-q`. The dissent's remedy is strictly worse for the property the dissent defends. **Weigh deepseek's Q5 dissent knowing its ring analysis was the panel's weakest:** its \":145 is a guest error -- the guest wrote more data than the ring can hold\" is factually off. The EMULATOR writes that ring on the guest's behalf; the defect is state representation. *Second time in two days that a seat-count in this project's records was wrong in the direction flattering the writer's own ruling. The tally was never the argument -- but a misquoted seat is a wrong record whichever way it points.*  *** ADJUDICATED 2026-08-25 (fable5, main-loop hat, on 8 of 9 mandated seats -- PROVISIONAL on its face w.r.t. the absent ninth, per that seat's own rule). *** **(1) Q5 UPHELD, two sharpenings.** Scalar per-device flag at :583 this round; deepseek's per-opcode set FILED. Per-opcode-versus-per-device is a SPEC choice, and deepseek's framing (\"the scalar IS the (d) mutant\") is circular -- it assumes per-opcode is the baseline and then calls the alternative a weakening. Clause (d) tests the CHOSEN spec's repair; it does not adjudicate between two acceptable specs. Put per-device in the CONTRACT (clause e) -- \"does not claim per-opcode uniqueness at :583\" -- so a later alternating-opcode observation is OUTSIDE the contract rather than a floor breach. **SHARPENING 1, the best single point of the panel: the two-different-opcodes row SHIPS ANYWAY, with the expectation INVERTED -- ONE LINE TOTAL.** Same stimulus deepseek wanted, opposite oracle. It kills a last-warned-opcode implementation (under which an alternating 0x5a/0xaa guest floods forever) AND pins the per-device spec in one row. **SHARPENING 2: the filed item inherits a TWO-membered framing and must not.** Honest weakening of my own precedent: #337's latched messages at :816 and :855 carry NO VALUE PAYLOAD -- :583 is the only site whose message names an opcode -- so \"three latches in this file\" is weaker precedent for discarding value-resolution than it looks. The filed option set is THREE-membered: scalar (shipped), per-opcode set (deepseek), and the in-tree COUNT-BOUND idiom (`n_trunc_msgs`, `src/cpus/memory_rw.c:495-523`) -- which this row already banners and which nobody in the dispute named. **(2) `wdcringambig` DOES NOT BLOCK.** The latch bounds the announcement to first occurrence; it does not silence it. **The corrupting store is ALREADY SILENT TODAY** -- :141-146 stores, advances, then tests, so the wrap line fires once per 262,656-byte lap and the N+1th store that destroys data prints nothing. What the latch removes is repetition of a late, miscorrelated warning. THREE OBLIGATIONS make it honest, all in-round and cheap: (a) /* #NNN */ comments at :145 and :160 naming `wdcringambig` -- \"latch bounds the announcement; the ring invariant is untouched\"; (b) the detector CONTRACT disclaims ring health, and the :145 primed-state row is marked for re-pin under the oracle-correction rule when `wdcringambig` lands; (c) file glm's dissent VERBATIM into `wdcringambig` as provenance. **THE TEMPTING THIRD PATH WAS CHECKED AND REJECTED FOR A MEASURED REASON.** grok and kimi both prefer restructuring :145 to the pckbc #288 shape (check-before-store, drop, latch -- verified in-tree at `dev_pckbc.c:158-167`). It must NOT fold in, and not merely by the reopening rule: **the restructure makes head==tail <=> empty sound ONLY on the byte path.** The bulk producer at :343-349 compares only against WDC_INBUF_SIZE and can land head exactly on tail with NO WARNING AT ALL (:876-879 mirrors it on tail). *pckbc had ONE producer; wdc has THREE.* A byte-path-only repair ships a half-invariant under a record that reads as soundness. **(3) SEQUENCING: `landiskdisk` SPLITS IN TWO.** The PROBE-level -d attach is part of `wdcflood`'s own witness battery and blocks nothing -- verified: :583 is unreachable without a disk (:461-468 early-returns), so the opus flood measurement necessarily ran disk-attached and the route is already proven. The BATTERY-RIG integration -- the half carrying the 28-34% NINSTRS poison -- is an independent deliverable and NOT a blocker. **The primed-head/tail row is a RUNG-1 DETECTOR row and cannot discharge :145's witness**; the 513-IDENTIFY route can, and was verified sound: no ring reset exists at command start (the only head/tail writes are :143, :165, :347-349, :876-879), IDENTIFY feeds wdc_addtoinbuf 512 bytes per command (:525-533), and 513 x 512 = 262,656 = WDC_INBUF_SIZE exactly. **(4) THE REPETITION RULE: DO NOT MINT, and my dichotomy was wrong on BOTH horns.** Clause (a) does NOT cover it -- (a) is DETECTOR-level and the defect is ROW-level: a one-off row at a latched site prints one line pre-fix and one line post-fix, colourless in both worlds, so it contributes nothing to (a) and hides behind rows that do. **But no new clause is needed, because clause (d) already forces it mechanically:** the fix adds six latch predicates, (d)-DEPENDS-ON mandates a null-repair mutant per predicate (flag never set), and the only row shape that reddens a nulled latch is one firing the same site twice with an EXACT count. Codex's sentence is the row-construction corollary of (d) for the latch class -- the same relation the accept-row failability rule bears to the de-escalation clause. Two overlapping statements of one rule is what the carrier refuses by name. **Disposition: one derivation sentence in the round block, not a carrier clause.** If a future latch round ships a SURVIVING one-off row, that measured escape is the (f)-style warrant to mint. **REFINEMENT ON THE ROWS (minimax): repetition with a \"<= 1 line\" oracle passes a mutant that prints ZERO. The oracle must be EXACTLY ONE, THEN ZERO** -- announcement AND suppression, which is the pair a latch actually promises.  *** PASS 2 (panel_20260825_204943) FOUND THREE ESCAPES AGAINST A DETECTOR MEASURED 14/14, AND ONE OF THEM IS A STRUCTURAL BLINDNESS OF THE ROWS RATHER THAN A GAP IN THEM. *** All three are L1 -- pre-ship construction, fixed in-round, not filed. **(1) glm -- THE RESET-ON-NORMAL-OPERATION ESCAPE, and it is the sharpest.** ONE line, reusing the existing flag, guard and branch, added inside an existing case for a VALID command: `d->warned_unimpl_cmd = 0;`. **Every site row fires its site TWICE IN SUCCESSION with no normal device operation between**, so the flag is never reset between fires and the row stays green -- while a real guest that probes an unimplemented opcode, issues IDENTIFY, probes again, issues IDENTIFY, floods forever. The same one-line trick applies at every one of the six sites. *This is the `#463` distinctness-invariance finding wearing new clothes: rows that test a property back-to-back are invariant under any edit that only changes what happens BETWEEN the two fires.* **(2) agy -- THE COVERAGE CENSUS, CONFIRMED AGAINST MY OWN PROBE.** \"The detector's six site rows do not test six distinct C sites\": `:145` has **ZERO** rows while its sibling `:160` has two, and `:583` has three. Also **the non-byte rows probe 16-bit ONLY** -- `mov.w` at both :607 and :1025, never `mov.l`, so a `len != 1` guard narrowed to `len == 2` would escape. **(3) deepseek -- `d->warned_X = !d->warned_X;`**, claimed to toggle so that fires 1 and 3 print while the detector only fires twice. **READING SAYS THIS DOES NOT WORK** -- the assignment sits INSIDE the guard (`if (!d->warned_unimpl_cmd) { d->warned_unimpl_cmd = 1; ...}`), so fire 2 never enters and the toggle never runs. **RECORDED AS A PREDICTION TO BE MEASURED, NOT AS REFUTED** -- this project settles disagreements by measurement, and the seat may be modelling a placement I have not considered. It goes in the second table either way, because the row that would kill it kills a whole CLASS. *** THE ROWS OWED, AND THE THIRD IS THE GENERAL ONE: *** `interleave` -- fire the site, issue a VALID command (0xEC IDENTIFY), fire again, demand ONE line; this is what kills glm's escape. `w32`/`r32` -- 32-bit non-byte access at :607 and :1025, closing agy's width gap. **`fire3` -- fire ONE site THREE times and demand exactly one line**, which kills the toggle class and any period-N latch at once, and is the cheapest row of the three. `:145` remains DISCLOSED-NOT-COVERED in the contract; agy's point stands but the contract already says so. *** AND AN HONEST RECORD ABOUT PROCESS: THE CARRIER'S PRE-TABLE CHECK WOULD HAVE CAUGHT agy's FINDING AND I DID NOT RUN IT. *** Its FIRST item is SIBLING PARITY -- *for each arm the diff touches, compare its observable channels against its nearest sibling arm; an asymmetry is a row you owe*. `:145` with zero rows beside `:160` with two IS that asymmetry, visible by counting, before any mutant was built. The check exists precisely because a table re-run is expensive, and this round is now paying that cost: the first table was measured against a 14-row detector and adding rows changes other mutants' scores, so a SECOND table is owed. **The check costs three greps and about thirty minutes; skipping it cost a table.**  *** THE FULL PASS-2 PANEL (8 of 8 answering; the kimi CLI walled and COVERED BY ITS SUBSTITUTE -- panel.sh reported that correctly for the first time) FOUND AN L2 FLOOR BREACH, TWO WRONG RECORDS IN SHIPPED TEXT, AND A DESIGN CLAIM WITH NO ROW BEHIND IT. *** **(L2 -- FLOOR BREACH, clause (b), grok) `:145` HAS NO DETECTOR ROW, SO THE FIX HAS SIX FIXED SITES AND THE DETECTOR SEES FIVE.** Clause (b) requires failure on a verbatim reinstatement at EVERY fixed site; reinstating `:145`'s unlatched form escapes clean. grok's verdict was DO-NOT-SHIP-AS-A-SIX-SITE-FLOOR and it is right. **Confirmed by counting my own probe: the six site-x2 rows cover FIVE sites -- `P_OVERRUN` appears only in `disjoint`, which fires an unimplemented command and never touches the ring.** The 8/14 -> 14/14 colour change is five tested sites plus `unimpl 2op`. It catches FIVE unique sites. **(L4 -- `disjoint` PROVES LESS THAN ITS NAME, codex.** It exercises only the unimplemented-command transcript, so it proves THAT stimulus matches one regex -- not that all six patterns map uniquely. The row's note overstates it. *** (DESIGN CLAIM WITH NO ROW, codex -- and this is the one I would have missed for good.) 14/14 DOES NOT ENFORCE SIX INDEPENDENT FLAGS. *** Every site test starts a NEW emulator (15 `session()` calls), so **pairing the read/write width flags, or the two ring flags, would pass every isolated row -- as would a PROCESS-GLOBAL latch**, unless one session exercises two controller instances. The round's headline design decision -- six flags, not four -- is therefore UNMEASURED by its own detector. codex adds the mitigating half, checked: the implementation does use six separately initialised per-instance fields, `DEVINIT(wdc)` zeroes the whole allocation, and none is reset or aliased later. **So the CODE is right and the DETECTOR cannot prove it** -- which under clause (e) must be disclosed, not claimed. **(ESCAPE, codex) An inserted `return 1;` immediately after the non-byte-read `fatal()`** leaves the FIRST 16-bit read's buffer untouched while the second runs normally -- and `r16 x2` checks only the line count. Needs a VALUE oracle, not a count. **(ESCAPE, ollama_kimi) `idata = data[0]` at the write-width site is a FOURTH guest-visible side effect** and has no pin row; my Q2 list named three. *** THE EPOCH-LATCH QUESTION (ollama_kimi and minimax, independently) IS ANSWERED AND THE ANSWER IS NO. *** They asked whether a guest-reachable device reset re-zeroes the `warned_*` flags, which would make the shipped code an epoch latch no row can see. Checked by reading: the only `memset(d, 0, sizeof(struct wdc_data))` is at :1126, immediately after the malloc at :1125 in DEVINIT. `COMMAND_RESET` (:484) and `ATAPI_SOFT_RESET` (:601) exist and neither re-zeroes the struct. **A real question, cheaply settled, and worth recording as a negative result so nobody re-asks it.** **(RECORDS, minimax) MY OWN BRIEF'S ROW ARITHMETIC DID NOT ADD UP:** it enumerated 2 controls + 6 site + 3 accept + 1 disjoint = 12 against a stated 14. The claim is true and this project settled it on 2026-08-21 by reading `core/debugmsg.c:384-390`, but the comment should CITE that rather than lean on the adjacent no-early-out fact. A fair catch. **(USEFUL FOR `wdcringambig`, ollama_kimi) A WASTE-ONE-SLOT DISAMBIGUATOR IS NOT FORBIDDEN BY CAPACITY:** 512 x 512 = 262,144 usable is still <= 262,655, so a max-length sector transfer still fits. Only the 513-undrained-IDENTIFY fill exceeds it. **Any future claim that \"waste-one breaks the exact-fit sizing\" is wrong**, and that is worth carrying to the ring round before it invents the objection.  *** KILL TABLE 1 -- 8 MUTANTS AGAINST THE 14-ROW DETECTOR, AND ONE ESCAPED. *** Preserved at `regress/artifacts/wdcflood/table1_14rows/` (12 files + a README stating what the numbers mean and why the directory is stamped). SCORES, every reddened row re-extracted with an ANCHORED trailing-`FAIL` match: `base` 14/14 (identity -- the harness scores the unmutated copy exactly as SEC does); `Mb1` 12/14 delete the :583 guard -> `unimpl x2`, `unimpl 2op`; `M1` 12/14 null the repair, flag never set -> same two; `M2` 11/14 SET BEFORE ANNOUNCE, zero lines -> those two plus `disjoint`; `M3` 13/14 last-warned-opcode -> **`unimpl 2op` ALONE**; `M4` 13/14 ABRT moved inside the guard -> **`abrt 2nd` ALONE**; `M5` 13/14 `return -1` moved inside the guard -> **`overread -1` ALONE**; `M6` **14/14 -- ESCAPED, ZERO ROWS REDDENED**. *** THE THREE SINGLE-ROW KILLS ARE THE USEFUL RESULT, NOT THE SCORES. *** `M3`, `M4` and `M5` each redden exactly ONE row, and those three rows exist for exactly those escapes -- nothing else in the file covers them. `abrt 2nd` and `overread -1` are the two rows that read a guest-visible SIDE EFFECT rather than a line count, and they are the only rows that can see a repair swallowed by a guard. *** M2 IS THE MEASURED VINDICATION OF AN ORACLE CHOICE. *** It sets the flag before the test so the site emits ZERO lines. **A `<= 1 line` oracle would have passed it**; `exactly 1` catches it. Every row in table 1 fires its site TWICE IN SUCCESSION, so nothing between the fires ever puts the operand back and the mutant looks perfectly latched. A guest that writes `seccnt = 0` between wide writes floods without bound. **This is a STRUCTURAL blindness of the row design, not a missing mutant** -- the same shape as `#463`'s distinctness invariance, where rows that assert a property back-to-back cannot see an edit that changes only what happens BETWEEN the assertions. *** DETECTOR NOW 22 ROWS, MEASURED 22/22 ON THE FIXED BUILD (`table2_22rows/base_22rows.raw`). *** Seven rows added, each closing something table 1 or pass 2 exposed: `overrun x2` (the :145 row -- **this is what closes the L2 floor breach**, and it needed a hardware loop, `dt r3` / `bf` with a computed displacement, because 513 undrained IDENTIFYs unrolled would be 513 debugger commands); `interleave` (fire, VALID command, fire); `w16 reset` (fire, guest CLEARS the operand, fire -- the M6 killer); `fire3` (kills period-N latches, not just period-2); `w32 x2` and `r32 x2` (the width rows probed 16-bit only, so a guard narrowed to `len == 2` escaped); `r16 value` and `w16 value` (value oracles at the two width sites, for a `return` inserted after a latched `fatal()` -- which a count-only row cannot see). All three anchors were verified to occur EXACTLY ONCE in the source first -- **a mutant that fails to apply scores a false clean**, which is the worst possible failure for a kill table. *** A CAPTION DEFECT IN THE APPARATUS, FOUND AND FIXED AT SOURCE. *** `killwdc.sh` wrote its header at start with the row count and the pre-fix score as LITERALS (`14 rows`, `8/14`). MECHANISM, read from the source rather than guessed: `strip_lits()` does NOT preserve offsets -- a string literal of any length becomes a single space, and a `/* ... */` block becomes a single space WITH ALL ITS NEWLINES DESTROYED.  The scan then runs over the stripped text while the line number is computed as `raw[:m.start()].count(chr(10))` -- an offset into the STRIPPED string indexed against the RAW one. The seat declined to supply a remembered layout and said so, which is what was asked of it.\n  * every WDC instance this file initialises registers the region TWO BYTES wide; the machine-by-machine floppy-attachment census is outside the permitted sources.\n\n**AND OPTION (b) -- return a constant `0x80` -- IS WEAKER THAN IT LOOKS:** *\"'Undefined' does not mean 'must be one'\"*, and on the active-low premise I supplied, **zero select bits ASSERT signals**, so an otherwise-zero byte is not a neutral default. What is unknown is whether drive and head suffice for EVERY defined bit.\n\n*** DISPOSITION: HOLD, PENDING AN ATA SPECIFICATION. ***  Preserving the current behaviour while the evidence is obtained is defensible; **declaring it hardware-correct is not**, and neither is inventing a byte. The same move is available here.\n\n**A NOTE FOR WHOEVER RESUMES THIS: if the disposition becomes (c), the existing witness INVERTS.**  It currently passes by showing INVARIANCE across head select (51 at head=0 and head=15).  Under (c) invariance becomes the FAILURE condition, so the round owes a positive oracle asserting the specified encoding -- and a row that merely shows the value CHANGED would be satisfied by any head-dependent garbage.\n\n*** SETTLED 2026-09-11 BY FETCHING ATA-2, AND IT REFUTES ALL FOUR OF MY OPTIONS INCLUDING THE ONE THE REVIEW CALLED THE ONLY DEFENSIBLE ONE. ***\n\n`_scratchpad/ata2_x3t10_948d.pdf` / `.txt` -- **Working Draft X3T10/0948D, \"Information Technology -- AT Attachment Interface with Extensions (ATA-2)\"**, 409 KB -> 314 KB of text, 5,909 lines. It is recommended that a device not respond to a read of this\n     address.  If a device does respond, it shall be sure not to drive the DD7 signal to prevent\n     possible conflict with floppy disk implementations.\"\n\n**SO THE DISPOSITION IS NOT TO ANSWER AT ALL**, and every option I put to the review is wrong:\n  * **(a) keep the status alias** and **(d) decline-in-place** -- identical to each other, as the review found, and both RESPOND where the standard recommends not responding;\n  * **(b) return a constant** -- responds, and invents a value the standard declines to specify;\n  * *** (c) MODEL DRIVE AND HEAD FAITHFULLY -- which the review called the only disposition that could be right -- IS REFUTED OUTRIGHT. ***  It would implement, carefully, a register the standard marks OBSOLETE and tells devices not to answer.  **The review could not have known this: it was confined to two files that do not contain the layout, and it said so.**\n\n*** AND THE MODEL SATISFIES THE DD7 CONSTRAINT ONLY BY ACCIDENT, WHICH IS A LATENT HAZARD WORTH ITS OWN SENTENCE. ***  `WDCS_BSY` is `0x80` -- exactly DD7 -- and `status_byte()` never sets it, assembling only DRDY, DSC, DRQ and ERR.  So today's alias happens to leave bit 7 clear and happens not to conflict with a floppy controller.  **Nothing enforces that.**  An edit that made `status_byte()` report BSY -- an entirely reasonable thing for a future round to do -- would start driving DD7 at an address the standard reserves against floppy conflict, and no row anywhere would notice.\n\n**WHAT THE ROUND NOW OWES, and it is a design question rather than a documentation one:** what \"not respond\" MEANS in this emulator.  Returning 0 from a device access function is not silence -- `memory_rw.c` converts it into an architectural exception on MIPS and M88K -- so the round must choose between an unanswered access, an open-bus value, and leaving the region registered one byte narrower so the address is never claimed.  **The narrowing option is the one that matches the standard's language most closely and is the only one that also fixes the write half by construction.**\n\n**THE WITNESS SURVIVES AND ITS ORACLE INVERTS THE OTHER WAY FROM WHAT I EXPECTED.**  I noted above that under (c) the invariance test would become a failure condition.  Under the real disposition the invariance is still not the point: the row must assert that the address is NOT ANSWERED with a status byte, and a new row should pin **bit 7 clear** so the accidental compliance above becomes an enforced one.\n\n*** THE IMPLEMENTATION IS DESIGNED AND IT IS ONE NUMBER: register `wdc_altstatus` with length **1 INSTEAD OF 2**.  2026-09-11 (wdcaltdigin/codex_impl_RETRY_20260911.txt). ***\n\n**THE OTHER THREE CANDIDATES ALL FAIL, and for different reasons worth keeping:**\n  * **(b) keep two bytes, return `0` at offset 1** -- *\"It implements a FAILED MAPPED ACCESS.  It cannot be identified with ATA nonresponse without a justified bus translation.\"*  WDC still claims the address; the guest gets an architectural exception on MIPS/M88K, which asserts something about the hardware that nothing establishes.\n  * **(c) keep two bytes, answer with a constant leaving DD7 clear** -- *\"No.  WDC still supplies a SUCCESSFUL response.\"*  It removes the status alias and preserves the thing Note 1 actually recommends against: answering at all.\n  * **(d) move the `status_byte()` call inside the offset-0 branch** -- leaves offset 1 answering the initialised zero, which is still a response.\n\n**EVERY ATTACHING MACHINE REGISTERS TWO BYTES TODAY** -- `MACHINE_MACPPC` at `addr + 0x160`, `MACHINE_HPCMIPS` at `0x14000386` and via its default formula, `MACHINE_IQ80321` at `addr + 0x402` -- and **no alternate-register functionality needs the second byte**: it supplies only the read alias and the guarded write, and the header already compiles `wd_digin` out.\n\n*** THE HONEST LIMIT, STATED BY THE SEAT AND KEPT HERE: narrowing removes WDC's CLAIM to the address, but what the COMPLETE MACHINE then does at an unclaimed address is not established by the permitted files. ***  That is a measurement, not a reading -- and it may differ by architecture, since `memory_rw.c` delivers a failed-device exception on MIPS and M88K only.  **The round owes that measurement on each attaching machine before it can claim 'does not respond' rather than merely 'WDC no longer answers'.**\n\n*Note the pleasing consequence if it holds: narrowing fixes the WRITE half by construction too, since an unclaimed address cannot carry a guarded write -- so the earlier write-side fix becomes redundant rather than wrong, and the round should say which it is.*\n\n*** OWNER RULING 2026-09-11: NARROW THE REGISTRATION. ***  Put to the owner as three options (narrow / open-bus constant / hold-plus-DD7-guard); the owner chose NARROW.  The site is `dev_wdc.c:2028-2029`, a literal `2` -- **and a harvest seat verified it is a literal `2 -> 1`, NOT `1 * addr_mult`**: the alt callback never divides its offset by `addr_mult`, and all five machine cases at `:2003-2018` change the ADDRESS only, never the length.\n\n*** MY OWN PREMISE IN THE OPTION SET WAS WRONG, AND I CORRECT IT HERE BEFORE IT IS ACTED ON. ***  I told the owner narrowing gives `MIPS/M88K: architectural bus fault`.  **It does not.**  I carried the carrier's TRUE statement about the REGISTERED-device `res <= 0` path (`memory_rw.c:603-611`, which does raise EXCEPTION_DBE) into the UNMAPPED path, which does not share it.  Read at `memory_rw.c:676-712`:\n  * an unmapped READ does `memset(data, 0, len)` and returns ZEROES;\n  * an unmapped WRITE falls straight to `do_return_ok`, silently ignored -- `:709-710` literally asks *\"Shouldn't there be a DBE exception for invalid writes as well?  TODO\"*;\n  * the MIPS `EXCEPTION_DBE` block at `:698-704` is inside `#if 0` -- **disabled**;\n  * `memory_warn_about_unimplemented_addr` (`core/memory.c:598-606`) returns false outright for `paddr < physical_max + 0x40000`, so there is no warning flood from an I/O-range address.\n\n**So narrowing is SAFER than I represented it, not riskier** -- no fault, no infinite loop, no flood. This is the fifth instance of the scope-transfer class in two days, and the first where the wrong address was carried into a question put to the OWNER.\n\n**THE `#494` MEASUREMENT DOES NOT TRANSFER EITHER, AND A SEAT REFUSED TO LET IT.**  The comment at `dev_wdc.c:655-665` records 19,793 measured `device 'wdc_altstatus' ... failed` lines and a guest looping forever in silence.  That is a REGISTERED device returning 0 -- the `res <= 0` path -- not the unassigned path narrowing produces.  The seat declined to fill the gap with a guessed per-architecture matrix and said the permitted files do not establish narrowing is safe on **every** attaching machine (MACPPC, IQ80321, HPCMIPS unverified).  *That refusal is the correct form and the residual is real: the generic path is now verified by reading, the per-machine claim is not.*\n\n*** TWO OBLIGATIONS THE NARROWING CREATES, BOTH FOUND BY THE SEAT AND NEITHER IN MY OPTION SET: ***\n  1. **A SPANNING ACCESS IS UNRESOLVED.**  The callback assigns only `data[0]`.  Nothing in the permitted files establishes whether a transaction starting at `A` and spanning the new one-byte boundary is SPLIT, ACCEPTED or REJECTED.  A 16-bit access at the ALTSTATUS address is the obvious guest shape, so **the byte-access argument for narrowing is not proof about those transactions** and this must be measured before the edit ships.\n  2. **THE DD7 ROW I PROPOSED WOULD BE VACUOUS AFTER NARROWING, AND ITS PREMISE IS WRONG ANYWAY.**  *Driving DD7 low is still driving DD7* -- bit-7-clear is not the electrical equivalent of leaving it undriven.  Once the device never answers, a conditional *\"if WDC answers, bit 7 is clear\"* row can never fire, and an UNCONDITIONAL assertion about the guest's value is simply wrong: another responder or the unassigned value may legitimately set it.  **The replacement oracle is ZERO WDC CONTRIBUTION, PROVEN BY ROUTING** -- address, direction and width observed at dispatch.\n\nKEEP the existing write-side guard as defence against a future wider registration, and correct its comment: the ATA-2 draft's write entry for this address is **\"Not Used\"**, not \"read-only\".\n\n*** 2026-09-11 -- SPANNING ACCESS: SAFE.  THE BLOCKER ON THE OWNER-RULED NARROWING IS CLEARED, AND THE EDIT ADDITIONALLY RETIRES A FILED DEFECT BY CONSTRUCTION. ***\n\nThe dispatcher ACCEPTS a wide access whole against the shorter region and CLAMPS it: `memory_rw.c:495-524`.  The `#95` pre-zero at `:491-492` runs first with the caller's FULL `len`, so the guest-visible buffer is byte-for-byte `{status, 0...}` before and after the change.  **No out-of-bounds read or write in either direction.**\n\n*** AND THE SEAT CORRECTED ITS OWN FIRST ANSWER, WHICH IS THE PART WORTH KEEPING. ***  It had called a wide access THEORETICAL.  It is reachable in-tree: `dev_ps2_spd.c:73-77`, `case 0x5c` (aux control), relays the guest's **own `len`, unmodified**, to `d->wdcaddr + 0x206` -- and the chain is live, not `#if 0`'d (`:44` `DEV_PS2_SPD_LENGTH 0x800`; `:103` `d->wdcaddr = devinit->addr + DEV_PS2_SPD_LENGTH`; `:111-113` auto-adds the wdc; `machine_playstation2.c:106` adds `ps2_spd addr=0x14000000`).  `MACHINE_PLAYSTATION2` is absent from `dev_wdc.c:2006-2018`'s switch, so the default arm puts `alt_status_addr` at exactly the address `case 0x5c` targets.\n\n**That STRENGTHENS the verdict rather than weakening it.**  The safety never depended on nobody issuing a wide access -- it follows from the clamp plus the handler touching only `data[0]`.  *A reachable path that is behaviourally identical is a better answer than an unreachable one.*  And it is likely rather than merely possible: the sibling arm at `:61-71` maps the command block at **2-byte spacing**, the signature of a 16-bit-only ATA host.\n\n*** THE NARROWING CLOSES `wdcaltwidth` BY ELIMINATION. ***  With `length == 1` the found-test forces `relative_addr == 0`, and the clamp then forces `len = 1` for every `len >= 2`, so **the handler can never again be entered with `len != 1`.**  `wdcaltwidth`'s named mutant (`idata = data[0]` -> `data[len - 1]`, which *\"escapes all 103 rows\"*) becomes EQUIVALENT once `len == 1`: `data[len-1]` IS `data[0]`.  **The class is dead, not merely uncovered.**  Two standing self-declared blind spots go with it -- `wdc_altoffset_probe.py:114-115` and `CHANGELOG.md:8617-8620` both describe a `len == 2` store at offset 0 that the narrowing makes unreachable.\n\n**PER THE ABSENCE-RECORD RULE, THE WARRANT IS NAMED: *the clamp at `memory_rw.c:523` forces `len == 1` for a one-byte region.*  Any future edit restoring the region's width MUST re-run the death measurement.**  This is closure by ELIMINATION, not by coverage, so the three overtaken-closure conditions do not apply -- but it does supersede the `ps2_spd` exposure, which is the one live consumer of the width the edit removes.\n\nTWO REFINEMENTS TO MY OWN CORRECTION, both accepted:\n  * the unmapped path is silent about DATA but **not about LOGGING** -- `memory_rw.c:676-679` calls `memory_warn_about_unimplemented_addr()`, which prints at `VERBOSITY_WARNING` while the default is `VERBOSITY_INFO`.  *(The suppression I cited, `core/memory.c:605`, applies below `physical_max + 0x40000`; whether this address qualifies is machine-dependent and is NOT settled here.)*  The `EXCEPTION_DBE` at `:701-703` is confirmed dead (`#if 0` at `:685`).\n  * **the safety is CONDITIONAL ON THE HANDLER'S SHAPE.**  It holds because `wdc_altstatus` touches only `data[0]` (`dev_wdc.c:614`, `:746-747`).  The sibling `DEVICE_ACCESS(wdc)` DOES inspect width and latches warnings at `:1093` and `:1968` -- **if anyone brings `wdc_altstatus` into line with that idiom, re-derive this.**\n\nNO SHIPPED DETECTOR ROW CHANGES COLOUR: `killtable_20260830/PRETABLE.out:49-54` already measured width-1 *\"green on every row\"*.  No oracle re-pin owed -- and equally, **no coverage for the change**, which is what the round still owes.\n\n**STATUS: the edit is a literal `2` -> `1` at `dev_wdc.c:2029`, owner-ruled, blocker cleared.  It owes a panel pass 1 on the actual diff and a detector row before it lands** -- the disposition being ruled does not discharge the lifecycle.\n\n*** `wdcaltdigin` PASS 1, 2026-09-11 (codex GPT-6 Astra at max, 15,433 b, complete).  **APPROVED** as a withdrawal of the WDC auxiliary decode at offset 1 -- and two of my premises are refuted, one of them the round's planned oracle. ***\n\n*** THE WITNESS DOES NOT INVERT -- INVARIANCE PASSES BOTH VERSIONS. ***  I had recorded that the existing head-invariance witness INVERTS under the fix.  It does not:\n\n    observable                     two-byte (now)   one-byte (fixed)\n    A+1 read, head 0                    0x51             0x00\n    A+1 read, head 15                   0x51             0x00\n    SDH readback after head 15          0xaf             0xaf\n    EQUALITY ACROSS HEADS               True             True\n\n> **Equality across heads still passes BOTH versions, so invariance alone cannot detect restoration of the two-byte registration.**  An exact `0x51` alias witness would simply stop observing the alias.  **SDH readback establishes that the head write landed in the main bank; it does NOT establish who answered `A+1`.**\n\n**So the acceptance criterion must be replaced with ROUTING EXCLUSION**, and head selection kept only as historical context or a control.  *With another responder present, even post-fix head invariance is not an appropriate requirement at all.*\n\n*** AND I ATTRIBUTED THAT WITNESS TO THE WRONG FILE. ***  `regress/wdc_altoffset_probe.py:113` states explicitly that **no row reads either alternate-region offset**; it contains offset-1 WRITE tests.  The seat declined to authenticate the head-select measurements from it and **refused to infer another filename from the defect id** -- which is precisely the discipline my own `dev_crime.c` error broke four hours earlier, applied back to me.\n\n**THE DD7 ORACLE IS VACUOUS BY CONSTRUCTION, with the reason made exact:** `status_byte()` adds only DRDY, DSC, DRQ and ERR, **so its aliased result ALREADY has bit 7 clear.  A bit-7-clear detector would accept the current behaviour.**\n\n**AND THE ANSWER TO \"IS RETURNING ZERO A RESPONSE?\" IS THE RIGHT FRAMING:** *\"The emulator completes a read with a numeric result; that does not mean WDC supplied that result.  Removing WDC's address claim is an honest implementation of device NONPARTICIPATION at this emulator's decoding abstraction.  **It does not simulate electrical high impedance.**\"*  The round may claim adoption of a draft RECOMMENDATION with the abstraction stated -- no more.\n\n=== SEVEN DETECTOR ROWS PROPOSED, each with its named mutant and the observable that differs ===\n`A1_READ_WDC_UNCLAIMED` and `A1_WRITE_WDC_UNCLAIMED` against `M_REWIDEN` (callback count 0 -> 1; **WDC state alone would miss the write mutant, since offset-1 write handling is already a no-op**); `A0_READ_ALTERNATE_STATUS` against suppression and against a constant `0x51`; `A0_WRITE_SRST_SIGNATURE` against `M_OMIT_COMMAND_RESET`; two existing P1 accept rows reused.\n\n**THE ROW I HAD NOT CONSIDERED, and it is the sharpest: `A0_READ_PRESERVES_PENDING_IRQ`.**  ATA-2 `:1535` distinguishes Alternate Status from Status precisely in that it does NOT clear a pending interrupt -- and the normal Status handler **does** clear it at `dev_wdc.c:1938`.  So a mutant adding that clearing action to the Alternate Status path is invisible to any status-byte-only oracle: **the returned bytes need not differ at all.**\n\n*** TWO DETECTOR-PACKAGING DEFECTS IN THE SHIPPED PROBE, AND THE SECOND IS A CHECK THAT CANNOT FAIL: ***\n  1. **The existing probe's \"committed\" expectations describe the OLDER offset-1 write-reset defect**, which today's callback already rejects at `dev_wdc.c:622`.  **Reusing its unchanged baseline for this new before/after comparison would MIS-SCORE THE BASELINE.**\n  2. **`ALTOFF_DET_DONE` is emitted UNCONDITIONALLY, and failed comparisons produce no failing exit status** (`wdc_altoffset_probe.py:682`).  **So neither that sentinel nor ordinary process completion is evidence of detector acceptance.**  *This project's own rule is that a preserved log is verified by its completion sentinel -- and here is a sentinel that says DONE whether or not the rows passed.  Filed as its own finding.*\n\n**THE WIDTH RETIREMENT STANDS WITH A SHARPER WARRANT:** `memory_rw.c:492` zeroes the incoming read length **before** the clamp at `:523`, the callback uses the first byte, so a wide read leaves `{status, 0...}` and a wide write retains its first-byte effect.  After narrowing, every positive-length access reaches the callback at offset zero with `len == 1`, so `data[0]` and `data[len - 1]` are equivalent there.  **`:523` is the correct warrant** -- *and it does NOT establish that wide guest accesses disappeared, nor that all width behaviour is hardware-accurate.*\n\n*Premise correction: the initializer carries **three** machine-specific case labels, not the five I implied.  No all-machine clearance is establishable from the permitted files.*\n\n*** 2026-09-11 -- **I NEARLY FILED FIFTEEN PROBES AS DEFECTIVE AND THE DESIGN IS SOUND**.  A NEAR-MISS, RECORDED BECAUSE THE CENSUS LOOKED AUTHORITATIVE AND WAS MEASURING THE WRONG PROPERTY. ***\n\nA pass-1 seat observed, correctly, that `wdc_altoffset_probe.py` emits `ALTOFF_DET_DONE` **unconditionally** and that *\"neither that token nor ordinary process completion is sufficient evidence of detector acceptance.\"*  I verified both halves: the sentinel is outside any conditional, and **there is no `sys.exit` anywhere in the file**, so the process always exits 0 however many rows failed.\n\nI then censused all 93 probes in `regress/`: **15 emit a completion sentinel and never call `sys.exit`.**  That reads like a fifteen-instance defect class.\n\n*** IT IS NOT A DEFECT.  I CHECKED WHAT ACTUALLY CONSUMES THEM BEFORE FILING, AND THE GATE DISCARDS `rc` ON PURPOSE. ***  `gate_arm.sh` runs the probe as `... > \"$SGILOG\" 2>&1 || true`, then:\n  1. **SENTINEL FIRST**, with the reason in its own comment -- *\"a preserved log is verified by its completion sentinel, never by its existence: a killed run loses every row and looks exactly like a run that produced none\"*;\n  2. **CONTROLS BY NAME, not by count** -- *\"a green-row total lets a control go red while the sum stays acceptable, and a run whose controls died is an APPARATUS failure, which must not be read as a wall of product failures\"*;\n  3. **PER-ROW PASS, checked individually** rather than by a total, explicitly against the padded-column class;\n  4. **THE DENOMINATOR PINNED TO A LITERAL**, so a detector that loses rows cannot pass by shrinking.\n\n> **So the sentinel's contract is COMPLETION, not ACCEPTANCE, and it discharges that correctly.  Pass/fail comes from per-row content checks the gate performs itself.  The seat's sentence is literally true and describes the DESIGN rather than a defect.**\n\n**MY CENSUS KEYED ON A CORRELATE -- the absence of `sys.exit` -- INSTEAD OF ON THE CLASS: whether pass/fail is OBSERVABLE TO THE CONSUMER.**  It is, everywhere it matters, through a channel my pattern could not see.  *That is the deriver lesson for the third time today: **before writing a deriver, ask what makes a member a member** -- and if the answer is a property the regex does not test, the error is invisible because the output is still a plausible number.*\n\n*Recorded as a near-miss rather than a finding because the cost of the other outcome was real: fifteen phantom defects filed against working probes, each of which a future round would have had to disprove.  What stopped it was the two-minute check this project already mandates -- **open the consumer and ask what it does when the thing is FALSE** -- and the answer was that the consumer never asked the question I was auditing.*\n\n*** 2026-09-11 ASSESS (main loop, read from source at HEAD).  **#494 CLOSED THE WRITE CELL AND LEFT THE READ CELL OPEN IN THE SAME `if` -- so this is not an independent defect, it is the SYMMETRIC CELL of a repair this project already shipped, which is the `#499` shape the carrier names by its own number.** ***\n\n**THE HANDLER, `DEVICE_ACCESS(wdc_altstatus)` at `dev_wdc.c:609-675`, in the order it executes:**\n  * `:614`  `idata = data[0];`\n  * `:617`  `odata = status_byte(d, cpu);`  -- **UNCONDITIONAL.  The offset is not consulted.**\n  * `:619`  `if (writeflag == MEM_READ)` -> `debug(\"[ wdc: read from ALTSTATUS ... \")` and nothing else; the common tail then assigns `odata` into the caller's buffer.\n  * `:622`  `else if (relative_addr != 0)` -> the **`#494`** arm: a WRITE to offset 1 is declined silently and the whole action is refused.\n  * `:673`  `else` -> the genuine Alt Ctrl write path (`#481`'s `WDCTL_RST`).\n\n> *** THE READ PATH NEVER DECODES ITS OFFSET AT ALL.  `relative_addr` is tested in exactly ONE place, and that place is inside the `else` of `writeflag == MEM_READ`. ***\n\n**So a READ of offset 1 -- the Drive Address register, `0x3f7` on the PC layout -- returns the ALT-STATUS byte**, which is this row's filed claim, now confirmed from the source rather than asserted.\n\n=== THE CELL SPACE, AND IT IS THE WHOLE FINDING ===\nThe function's own inputs give a two-by-two: **{READ, WRITE} x {offset 0, offset 1}.**\n\n| cell | disposition at HEAD |\n|---|---|\n| WRITE, offset 0 | Alt Ctrl, correct (`#481`) |\n| WRITE, offset 1 | **CLOSED by `#494`** -- declined silently, whole action refused |\n| READ,  offset 0 | alt-status, correct |\n| READ,  offset 1 | *** OPEN -- returns the alt-status byte *** |\n\n**`#494` repaired the cell its mutant used and left the symmetric one.**  The carrier states this class in its own words -- *'the original breach, reproduced inside its own repair, by someone who had just spent an hour understanding it'* -- and prescribes the remedy: **when a class recurs, enumerate the full space from the function's own inputs and close every cell in ONE diff, then write into the contract which cells are covered.**  Four cells; three closed; one diff owed.\n\n*** AND `#494`'S OWN COMMENT CARRIES A MEASUREMENT THAT BEARS DIRECTLY ON THE REPAIR CHOICE, which is why it must be read before this round picks a disposition. ***  At `:655-665` it records, correcting an earlier wrong reason in place:\n\n> *'THE ACCESS STAYS HANDLED (`return 1`) ... this device is REGISTERED, so a 0 never reaches [unassigned-address] handling.  `memory_rw` sets `res = -1`, takes its `res <= 0` branch, and raises `EXCEPTION_DBE` -- measured, **19793 `device 'wdc_altstatus' ... failed` lines and ZERO `non-existant` ones** under `-v`.  The guest then loops forever re-executing the faulting store, and at DEFAULT verbosity it does so in SILENCE.'*\n\n**So `return 0` FROM THIS REGISTERED DEVICE IS MEASURED TO HANG THE GUEST SILENTLY.**  That is a fact about a *registered* device returning 0 -- it is NOT the same thing as narrowing the registration, which makes the address unmapped and takes an entirely different path.  *The two dispositions are easy to conflate and the tree has a measurement distinguishing them; it would be a wrong record to cite that 19793 against a narrowing.*\n\n=== THE OPTION SET FOR THE OPEN CELL, with what each actually does here ===\n  * **NARROW THE REGISTRATION** (`dev_wdc.c:2028-2029`, the literal `2` -> `1`) -- closes BOTH offset-1 cells structurally, and the handler's `relative_addr != 0` arm becomes dead code that must then be dispositioned rather than left as a comment describing a case that can no longer arrive.  **Changes 0x3f7 from HANDLED-AND-DECLINED to UNMAPPED**, so the `#494` measurement above does not transfer and the unmapped path must be measured on its own.\n  * **DECODE THE READ** -- mirror `#494` on the read side inside the handler, leaving the registration alone.  Smallest diff; keeps the address owned by wdc; makes the two-by-two symmetric.\n  * **BACKED ACCEPT / IMPLEMENT** -- model the Drive Address register.  **Refused by the tree's own evidence**: `wdcreg.h` compiles `wd_digin` out under an `#if 0` whose note reads *NOT MAPPED; fd uses this register on PCs*, and `dev_fdc.c`'s `DEV_FDC_LENGTH` carries *TODO 8, but collision with wdc*.  **The tree documents the collision from BOTH sides and resolves it from neither** -- which is [[wdcaltownership]], and it is a different question from this one.\n\n*** THE ORDERING CONSTRAINT, so a round does not ship the two in the wrong sequence: NARROWING SUBSUMES DECODING, and decoding first then narrowing leaves dead code behind. ***  [[wdcaltwidth]] is coupled to the same literal -- the region is registered two bytes wide while every existing row uses `len == 1`, so a narrowing also settles that row's unpinned width, and any detector written here should pin the width deliberately rather than inherit it.\n\n**STATUS: specified, NOT implemented.**  No edit made.  A build and a measurement are owed and could not be taken in this window -- a 24-leg regression tail holds the machine and this project's gates carry load-sensitive wall-clock oracles, so a measurement taken now would be worth nothing.  *The disposition question is put to the owner rather than assumed, because 'narrow the registration' and 'FAULT -- return 0' are different actions here and the tree has a measurement showing the second one hangs the guest.*\n\n*** 2026-09-11 DESIGN (codex GPT-6 Astra at max, 23,793 b, 0 cyber flags) + **OWNER RULING: DECODE THE READ, registration untouched.** ***\n\n*VERDICT: The four-cell table is correct.  The registered-device fault is live, but its measurement does not establish narrowing behavior.*\n\n**MY CELL TABLE SURVIVED THE SOURCE CHECK** -- the read path does not decode its offset, and the open cell is (READ, offset 1).  **And the transferability question I raised was answered the way I suspected: `#494`'s measured 19793 failed-access lines describe a REGISTERED device returning 0, and do NOT establish what a NARROWED registration would do.**  *Citing that measurement against a narrowing would have been a wrong record, which is why it was asked rather than assumed.*\n\n*** AND IT CAUGHT MY THIRD INVENTED PATH OF THE DAY. ***  The brief listed `src/include/wdcreg.h`; the seat reported *'The system cannot find the file specified'*, **did not guess a replacement** (as the brief instructed), and scoped its answer to the three files it could open.  The real path is **`src/include/thirdparty/wdcreg.h`**.  *Three invented paths in one session -- `dev_crime.c`, `regress/sh_tlb_probe.py`, and now this -- each caught by a seat that refused to substitute a neighbour.  The brief rule that produced the catch is the one that says VERIFY EVERY PERMITTED PATH EXISTS, and I verified four of five.*\n\n=== THE REPAIR, AS THE SEAT WROTE IT ===\n\nDecline the offset-1 read, return byte **`0x00`**, retain handler return value **1**, keep the unconditional status calculation and the common tail:\n\n    if (writeflag == MEM_READ) {\n        if (relative_addr != 0) {\n            odata = 0;\n        } else {\n            debug(\"[ wdc: read from ALTSTATUS: 0x%02x ]\" ..., (int)odata);\n        }\n    } else if (relative_addr != 0) {\n        /*  Existing #494 write-decline arm.  */\n\nThe Alt Ctrl arm follows unchanged.\n\n**ZERO'S WARRANT IS NAMED AND ITS LIMIT IS NAMED WITH IT** -- the emulator's deterministic declined-read convention: `#95` pre-zeroes registered-device read buffers and the ordinary unimplemented-address path also chooses zero.  *** 'That is an emulator policy, not proof that a real Drive Address or floppy register must read zero.' ***  *An honest warrant with its scope stated, rather than a hardware claim the files cannot support -- which is the distinction [[wdcaltownership]] turns on.*\n\n=== *** THE ORACLE TRAP, AND IT IS THE SAME SHAPE AS TONIGHT'S n6 FINDING *** ===\n\nThe clause-(d) mutant is **B-null-effect**: keep `if (relative_addr != 0)` and its branch, but remove `odata = 0`, so offset-1 reads return the status byte S again.\n\n> *** A DECLINED READ MUST USE A FIXTURE WITH **S != 0** TO KILL THAT MUTANT.  IF S IS ZERO, THE FIXED AND THE BROKEN IMPLEMENTATIONS RETURN THE SAME BYTE. ***\n\n**That is a check that cannot fail, identified before any row is written** -- the second one caught at design time tonight, after `n6`'s `r7 == 0` row would have been vacuous against a mask-returning variant for exactly the same reason (*the mask would also be zero*).  *Two independent rounds, same failure shape: an oracle asserting ZERO is worthless unless the defective path is shown to produce NON-zero.*\n\n**AND THE CONTROL IS ESTABLISHABLE, which is why this is a constraint rather than a blocker:** **disklessness does NOT imply zero status** -- initialisation sets `d->error = 1` at `dev_wdc.c:1994`, and `status_byte()` includes `WDCS_ERR` when `d->error` is set at `:594-595`.  *So a diskless fixture is a legitimate null medium AND can carry a nonzero S; the round must establish that explicitly rather than assume it.*\n\n**THE SECOND NULL-MEDIUM MEANING NEEDS ITS OWN REJECTION:** a null test vehicle that returns zero for every read without exercising wdc at all **is accepted by an offset-1-zero assertion alone**.  The detector must reject it through a **legal offset-0 read expected to return nonzero S**.\n\n*** A NOTE ON THE ROAD NOT TAKEN, recorded because the reasoning is reusable: for option A (narrowing) the seat observed that `2 -> 1` HAS NO GUARD-AND-EFFECT PAIR, so 'changing 1 back to 2' is REVERSION, not a clause-(d) mutation. ***  Its structural analogue would be **A-null-exclusion** -- keep the one-byte registration but add a SECOND one-byte registration of the released address back to this handler, defeating the exclusion while the original line still reads `1`.  **Checking only that the registration still says `1` would miss it.**  *Filed as reasoning for whoever eventually takes [[wdcaltwidth]]; not this round's problem.*\n\n**STATUS: specified and RULED. GXEMUL-SEC\tools\pipeline\ledger.json:28545:   "note": "*** THE WIDEST DOOR IN THIS DEVICE, AND NO LEDGER ROW COVERED IT.  Found by the adjudicating seat while ranking the OTHER four sgi_re rows; verified here by opening the file and deriving the gap arithmetic. ***  **THE SITE** is the final `else` of the window cascade in `DEVICE_ACCESS(sgi_re)` -- a `fatal()` for each direction and then a bare `exit(1)`, with **NO WIDTH PREDICATE AT ALL**.  Every other kill in this handler is reached only by choosing an unusual access WIDTH (the six `len != 8` arms) or a DIRECTION (the six read arms).  This one needs neither: any width, either direction, any unmatched address.  **THE DOOR, derived rather than asserted.**  `memory_device_register` covers `baseaddr + 0x1000` for `DEV_SGI_RE_LENGTH` = `0x1000`, and the handler restores the full offset with `relative_addr += 0x1000`, so the dispatched range is `0x1000-0x1fff`.  The six windows occupy `0x1000-0x17ff` with one internal gap:      TLB_A 0x1000-0x11ff   TLB_B 0x1200-0x13ff   TLB_C 0x1400-0x15ff      TEX   0x1600-0x16df   LINEAR_A 0x1700-0x177f  LINEAR_B 0x1780-0x17ff  leaving **`0x16e0-0x16ff` (32 bytes) plus `0x1800-0x1fff` (2048 bytes) = 2080 bytes** that fall through to the kill.  Guest KSEG1: a single `lb` at `0xb5001800` ends the host process.  *** IT SURVIVES BOTH ROUNDS THAT LOOK LIKE THEY WOULD FIX IT, WHICH IS WHY IT MUST BE FILED BEFORE THE NEXT COMPLETENESS SENTENCE SHIPS. ***  `#497` repairs the six WIDTH guards and never reaches this arm.  `sgirekills` as scoped repairs the six READ arms inside the windows and never reaches it either.  A reader of either round record could reasonably conclude the device no longer kills on a stray access.  It does.  **AND ITS FIX CANNOT BE ACCEPT**, which is what keeps it out of `sgirekills`' round rather than a scheduling preference: nothing backs an unmapped address, so there is no stored value to return.  It is DECLINE + a latched `(once)` announce -- a different fix design, so the round-scope rule's same-fix-design arm fails.  *The agy audit listed this arm as '1 unhandled offset fallback' and never asked whether it was reachable.  A census that enumerates a site without ranking it is how the widest door stays open.*  *** DESIGN SETTLED 2026-08-30 BY ADJUDICATION, BEFORE EITHER ROUND STARTS, SO NEITHER BURNS ITS PANEL ARGUING SCOPE. ***  **TWO ROUNDS, and both separate from `#497` -- all THREE round-scope conjuncts fail, worked through rather than asserted.**  SAME DEVICE fails: `sgi_de` and `sgi_re` are separate `memory_device_register` registrations at `baseaddr+0x2000` and `+0x1000`.  SAME SEMANTIC CLASS fails: `sgidewidth` is the WIDTH class with a real accept set `{4,8}` a decline must PRESERVE, while `sgireunmapped` is the BADOFFSET class where nothing legal reaches the arm at all.  **SAME DETECTOR / NO NEW ORACLE fails, and this is the one that decides it:** `sgi_de`'s registers are READABLE so its rows read back through `odata` -- a real device-signature channel -- while `sgi_re`'s else arm has NO BACKING STORE and its rows can only observe the diagnostic and the guest-gets-zero channel.  *Those are different oracles in kind, and the tempting collapse -- 'both are a refused access ending the host' -- is exactly what the no-new-oracle clause exists to refuse.*  *** THE CHANNEL FINDING, AND IT IS THE MOST IMPORTANT LINE IN THIS BLOCK. ***  `#497`'s `d_returnzero` escaped 35/35 and the reason generalises to BOTH these rounds: **the `(once)` announce prints IDENTICALLY for a correct decline and for a `return 0`**, because the announce happens before the return statement.  So the DIAGNOSTIC channel does not kill it either -- and neither does host survival, since a guest bus error leaves the host alive.  **The only discriminator is what the GUEST RECEIVED.**  Every round in this family owes a SENTINEL-SEEDED READ ROW: seed the destination register, perform the declined read, and assert it reads back 0 -- a correct decline delivers zero, `return 0` faults and the register keeps its seed.  *That is the row `#497` was missing, and the measuring seat reached the same repair independently while this adjudication was being written.*  **TWO LATCH BITS, NOT ONE, and the precedent is MEASURED rather than stylistic.**  The arm prints two distinct messages -- `unimplemented write to address` and `unimplemented read from address`.  `#490` built the one-bit form as `M3shared` and **it ESCAPED**: a session declining at both sites sees the second fall silent because the first already latched.  `#279` states the rule outright -- *one flag per DISTINCT MESSAGE and never one shared flag*.  A single bit here means the first stray READ silences the first stray WRITE's message forever, and vice versa.  **THE DE-ESCALATION ACCEPT CLAUSE IS N/A, WITH ITS REASON RECORDED -- not unrun.**  The clause enumerates predicates that used to gate death and pins each accept side.  This arm **has no predicate**: it is the fall-through when none of the six window conditions match, so there is no adjacent modelled value because **nothing legal reaches it**.  The enumeration is the empty set.  *Adding a row to test the WINDOW predicates instead would be the addition licence's 'cannot hurt' wearing detector clothes -- and it is unnecessary, because `#497`'s own accept rows already prove those addresses reach their own arms rather than this one.  That is inherited coverage, not an uncovered hole.*  **WHAT IT OWES INSTEAD IS A CONTRACT SENTENCE (clause (e)):** this fix makes every unmatched address a silent zero-decline, so a future window-boundary regression that drops a legal address here no longer announces -- and that class is pinned by `#497`'s window accept rows, not by this arm.  *** AND A CONTRACT LIMIT WORTH KNOWING BEFORE ROWS ARE WRITTEN: the WRITE arm's `return 0` mutant is NOT distinguishable by a value-only cold-debugger probe. ***  A declined write and a faulting write both leave no readback.  The READ arm is killable by the seeded-read row; the write arm needs exception-state observation, which is a heavier probe shape than `#497` used.  **State it as a limit rather than shipping a completeness claim the rows cannot support.**  *** THE 32-BYTE GAP IS NOT DEAD SPACE.  IT IS `CRIME_RE_CLIP_IDS`, A SEVENTH REAL HARDWARE WINDOW. ***  `crmfbreg.h` defines `CRIME_RE_CLIP_IDS 0x16e0` between `CRIME_RE_TEX 0x1600` and `CRIME_RE_LINEAR_A 0x1700`, and `struct sgi_re_data` carries `// todo: clip_ids registers.` exactly where they would go.  The codex seat reached the same conclusion from the CRIME spec and put it best: **\"six real windows\" should read \"six MODELED windows\"** -- CLIP_IDS is real hardware with no dispatch and no state.  **The 2080-address count is UNCHANGED** (CLIP_IDS still falls to the kill) **but the characterisation splits, and the decline message must follow it:** 32 bytes are a documented, unmodelled register block; 2048 are genuinely unmapped.  A single \"unimplemented address\" diagnostic is accurate for the 2048 and WRONG for the 32.  *The brief asked whether `CRIME_RE_TEX + 0xe0` was a bug or the gap was real; both options were wrong, and a seat answering as posed would have had to pick the wrong one of two.  Three local facts agree the bound is deliberate: the next defined constant, the struct's own todo comment, and `re_tex[112]` x 2 bytes = 0xe0 exactly.*  *** THE fable5 SEAT'S EQUIVALENCE CLAIM IS REFUTED, AND THE REFUTATION CHANGES A DISPOSITION RATHER THAN SCORING A POINT. ***  It reported that an early `return 1` after the announce is OBSERVATIONALLY EQUIVALENT because *\"`memory_rw.c`'s #95 block memsets `data` to 0 on EVERY device read before the handler runs\"*, and advised disposing of that mutant as EQUIVALENT rather than chasing it.  **Checked both halves and both are false.**  The device handler is called at `memory_rw.c` `res = mem->devices[i].f(cpu, mem, paddr, data, len, ...)` and NOTHING zeroes `data` before it -- the three `memset(data, 0, len)` sites in that file are all on NON-DEVICE paths (the MIPS Sprite area, the beyond-physical_max arm, and the `memblock == NULL` unmapped-RAM arm), each followed by a `goto do_return_ok`.  And the MIPS caller's buffer is `uint8_t data[LS_SIZE];`, an **UNINITIALISED STACK ARRAY** with no memset anywhere in that file.  **SO THE MUTANT IS NOT EQUIVALENT -- BUT THE CORRECT DISPOSITION IS NOT 'DETECTABLE' EITHER, AND THAT NUANCE IS THE POINT.**  Skipping the tail leaves the guest whatever was on the stack, which is neither 0 nor any fixed value: the observable is REAL but NONDETERMINISTIC.  A row asserting a specific sentinel would be FLAKY, not sound.  *Filing it as EQUIVALENT would have retired a live mutant on a false mechanism; filing it as ordinary coverage debt would invite a flaky row.  It is neither, and the round must say so.*  **The seat's own remedy for the sibling `return 0` case is the one that works here too: seed the destination register with a sentinel and prove execution continued past the load** -- which pins the mutant without depending on stack contents.  *Everything else in that answer verified: `dev_unreadable.c` IS `return 0;` and `machine_sgi.c` installs it TEN times, so the FAULT member is real and in use on this machine.*  *** THE PASS-1 PANEL RESOLVED THE ROUND'S ONLY DISAGREEMENT BY WARRANT, NOT BY VOTE. ***  grok said ONE latch bit, deepseek said TWO. So two bits is not a new rule, it is the existing rule counted correctly** -- and my grep found nothing because I searched for the wrong property. MY REFUTATION OF fable5's EQUIVALENCE CLAIM WAS WRONG, AND I READ THE PRISTINE 2021 UPSTREAM COPY OF `memory_rw.c` TO PRODUCE IT.  fable5 WAS RIGHT. ***  I wrote that *\"nothing zeroes `data` before the handler call -- the three `memset(data, 0, len)` sites are all on NON-DEVICE paths\"*, and shipped it in `a115a4a`.  **The fork's `src/cpus/memory_rw.c` has SIX such sites, and one of them is exactly what fable5 described**, inside the device-found branch, before `paddr -= baseaddr` and before the dispatch:  `/*  #95: zero the caller's buffer up front on a read so a device handler that fills only part of it ... can never hand back uninitialised host memory to the guest.  */  if (writeflag == MEM_READ) memset(data, 0, len);`  *** HOW IT HAPPENED, AND IT IS THE HAZARD THIS PROJECT DOCUMENTS IN CAPITALS FOR SEATS -- IT CAUGHT THE MAIN LOOP INSTEAD. ***  There are two `memory_rw.c` under the project root: the fork at **794 lines** (`GXEMUL-SEC`, `est`, `build`, `build-asan`, md5 3d47dd66) and a PRISTINE 2021 UPSTREAM copy at **530 lines** (`./src/cpus/`, md5 75fbe149, plus two archive copies).
