VERDICT: CONSTRUCTIBLE as a\nrung-3 witness on stock `alphabook1`.  It corrected FOUR of my premises, one of which was a wrong\nrecord in this row's own OUTSTANDING_BUGS entry. ***\n\n**1. THE RECOMMENDED FIX WAS WRONG, AND SO WAS ITS WARRANT.**  The entry said `0x%llx` with a\n`(long long)` cast, *\"the file's own idiom elsewhere\"*.  `dev_lca.c` uses **`PRIx64` five times and\n`%llx` never**, and the siblings **KEEP** the cast as `(uint64_t)idata`.  My design brief compounded\nit by saying to DROP the cast -- a third wrong form.  Corrected in place.\n\n**2. `load_const` CANNOT BUILD EITHER OPERAND.**  LDA/LDAH make 32-bit sign-extended constants; the\nK0SEG address needs bits 63..42 and the test value needs bit 32.  **A probe using `load_const` for\neither is not this witness.**  The working bootstrap is the one the Alpha harness already uses:\n`put d` the 64-bit words into RAM below 64 MB, then guest `ldq` them while `ptbr` is still 0.\n\n**3. THE MISS MODE IS NOT ONLY 'UNMAPPED -> NOTHING'.**  Offset 0 is `LCA_IOC_BASE` (HAE), which\n`break`s with no kill -- so a store to `k0(0x180000000)` is **silent while INSIDE the LCA window**.\nUnmapped and HAE both read back 0, which is why the control has to distinguish them.\n\n**4. TWO ROUTES THAT LOOK LIKE CONTROLS AND ARE NOT:**\n  * **debugger `dump` cannot read the register.**  `dump` calls `memory_rw` with `NO_EXCEPTIONS`, and\n    LCA is `DM_DEFAULT` rather than `DM_READS_HAVE_NO_SIDE_EFFECTS`, so the handler is **SKIPPED** and\n    the caller gets the pre-zeroed buffer -- *the unmapped signature, produced by a hit.*\n  * **`put d` of the killing value is not rung-3.**  `store_64bit_word` goes through `memory_rw` with\n    `CACHE_DATA` and no `NO_EXCEPTIONS`, so it DOES enter the handler and `exit(1)` from the\n    debugger -- a real kill, by no guest instruction.\n\n> **THE CONTROL THAT WORKS: a guest `stq` of `0x1` followed by a guest `ldq`, same decode, BEFORE\n> the killing store.**  It is the analogue of the Alpha harness's `swpctx mapped` row.  Neither it\n> nor the kill text is sufficient alone -- the round-trip proves the window is THIS arm and not HAE\n> or unmapped; the text `IOC_CONF: 0x0` proves the arm fired and that a 32-bit zero store could not\n> have produced that pair.\n\n**Q3 CONFIRMED IN DETAIL, and it hardens S2:** `stq` is opcode `0x2d` -> `loadstore_type 3` -> `LS_Q`;\n`host_store` is NULL for a device page, so it falls to `LS_GENERIC_N` and calls `memory_rw(..., 8,\nMEM_WRITE)`; the memblock granule is 1 MB so `0x20+8` crosses nothing; Alpha is little-endian and\n`memory_readmax64` rebuilds the value.  **And the guard's own arithmetic is load-bearing:** `~1` is a\nsigned `int` (-2) converting to `0xfffffffffffffffe`, **so it sees bit 32 -- `~1U` would NOT, and\nthat is a different mutant worth building.**  The same function already compares against\n`0x300800000ULL`, so 64-bit values are this handler's native width.\n\n**One honest weakening:** `(int)0x100000000 == 0` is IMPLEMENTATION-DEFINED, not guaranteed by C99.\nGCC/Clang truncate, so the witness holds on this build -- but the row should not claim a language\nguarantee.\n\n*** AND THE 309 FIGURE WAS ALREADY IN THIS ROW'S OWN ENTRY, DATED 2026-08-31. ***  I ran a census\ntoday, got 226, treated it as new, and was corrected to 309 by a seat -- while\n`OUTSTANDING_BUGS.md` had recorded **\"309 casts across 70 device files\"** five days earlier, with\nits deriver.  **The unread-review class, in the file I was writing about.**  (Its companion figure,\n45 host-kill sites, is not in conflict with today's 18: the two used different window sizes, and\nneither states one, which is the reason they cannot be reconciled.)\n\n*** WITNESS BUILT AND RUN THE SAME HOUR THE DESIGN LANDED.  HEAD 4/6. ***\n\n    live          alive   alive          ok    the control session survives the legal write\n    ioc rt        0x1     0x1            ok    guest stq 0x1 then guest ldq, SAME decode\n    kill fired    yes     yes            ok    the IOC_CONF arm announced -- not HAE, not unmapped\n    kill width    0x0     0x100000000    FAIL  the message must name the value that ACTUALLY killed\n    kill notzero  0x0     NOT 0x0        FAIL  0x0 is a value this guard ACCEPTS\n    died          died    died           ok    exit(1) really ran\n\nAll four of grok's design corrections are implemented and each is a comment in the probe: the\n64-bit operands are planted with `put d` and loaded with `ldq` (`load_const` cannot build them);\nthe read-back is a guest `ldq` (debugger `dump` would skip a `DM_DEFAULT` handler and hand back the\npre-zeroed buffer -- the unmapped signature from a hit); the killing store is a guest `stq` (a\n`put d` would kill from the debugger and not be rung-3); and `ioc rt` distinguishes the real arm\nfrom HAE at offset 0, which `break`s silently INSIDE the window.\n\n*** AND MY FIRST DRAFT HAD THE ORACLE POLARITY BACKWARDS -- it scored 6/6 on the committed build. ***\nIt asserted the DEFECT as the passing state, so it read as success and would have gone RED the\nmoment the bug was fixed.  **The ladder requires a witness to show the symptom pre-fix and go green\npost-fix WITH THE SAME SCRIPT**, which is clause (i).      <- EXPLICITLY STRIPPED\n                    byte_order = EMUL_LITTLE_ENDIAN;\n            }\n            ... for (i=0; i<len; i++) { x <<= 8; x |= buf[i]; }\n\n**The flag is stripped from `len` before it is used as a count, so it cannot change the length.**\nAnd the two cannot collide in practice: the flag is 128 while access widths are 1..8.\n\n**WHAT THAT LEAVES, and it is now the WHOLE question:** `x` accumulates exactly `len` bytes, so\n\n    len == 4  ->  x <= 0xffffffff, bits 32..63 are ZERO  ->  the sign-extended ones meet only\n                  zeros and NOTHING OBSERVABLE HAPPENS\n    len == 8  ->  x can carry bits 32..63                ->  the spurious dev==31 match is REAL\n\n> **So the row turns entirely on: can a guest issue an 8-BYTE access to `0xe2800000` on macppc, and\n> does it reach this handler with `len == 8` intact?**  Nothing else matters -- not the endianness\n> flag, not the arithmetic, both of which are now settled.\n\n*Recorded because narrowing a question is worth as much as answering one, and because this half was\nsettled by reading two functions while a seat works the other half.  The codex analysis in flight is\nasked the same thing independently; if it disagrees about the stripping, the disagreement is\ncheckable in four lines.*\n\n*** ANSWERED: **NO**.  THE ROW IS DOWNGRADED med -> low, AND THE INVESTIGATION IS WHY THE SEVERITY\nWAS LEFT UNGRADED IN THE FIRST PLACE. ***\n\ncodex: *\"The macppc guest-store paths cannot place bits 32..63 in this register's `idata` or\n`cur_addr`: the dispatcher clamps the four-byte address window before calling the handler.\n`MEM_PCI_LITTLE_ENDIAN` preserves these access widths.\"*  **Both halves verified here rather than\naccepted:**\n\n    dev_uninorth.c:141-142   memory_device_register(mem, \"uninorth_pci_addr\", addr + 0x800000,\n                                 4, dev_uninorth_addr_access, d, DM_DEFAULT, NULL);\n                                 ^-- REGISTERED LENGTH 4\n\n    memory_rw.c:495          if (paddr + len > mem->devices[i].length)   <- truncates, with the\n                                 fork's own #91/#93/#94 per-device note above it\n\nSo an 8-byte guest store to `0xe2800000` is **truncated to 4 bytes before the handler runs**.\n`memory_readmax64` then accumulates at most 4 bytes, `idata <= 0xffffffff`, bits 32..63 are ZERO,\nand the sign-extended ones in `0xffffffff80000000` meet only zeros.  **Nothing observable happens.**\n\n> **SO THIS COLLAPSES INTO THE SAME LANGUAGE-LEVEL HYGIENE AS THE OTHER TWO [[shiftubcensus]]\n> MEMBERS.**  The shift is still UB and still worth the one-token repair; what is retracted is the\n> claim that it mis-decodes a device.\n\n*** THE PROCESS POINT IS THE VALUE HERE. ***  The arithmetic was right, the device really is\ninstantiated on a supported machine, and the sign-extension really would select the wrong device --\n**every step held except reachability, and reachability is what decided it.**  Had the row been\ngraded HIGH on the arithmetic alone it would have carried a guest-visible claim that is false, and a\nfix round would have been designed around a witness that cannot exist.  *Leaving the number blank\ncost one codex invocation and bought a correct grade.*\n\n**A DETAIL WORTH KEEPING:** the sibling `uninorth_pci_data` port at `+0xc00000` IS registered with\nlength **8** (`:143-144`).  So the width concern is not absurd in general for this device -- it is\nsimply wrong for the ADDRESS port, which is the one with the shift.  A future reader tempted to\ngeneralise \"uninorth clamps to 4\" should not."

187:     def rd(self, t=0.4):
188:         r, _, _ = select.select([self.fd], [], [], t)
189:         if self.fd not in r:
190:             return True
191:         try:
192:             d = os.read(self.fd, 65536)
193:         except OSError:
194:             self.dead = True
195:             return False
196:         if not d:
197:             self.dead = True
198:             return False
199:         self.buf += d.decode("latin1", "replace")
200:         return True
201: 
202:     def wait(self, mark=0, echo=None, timeout=60):
203:         t = time.time()
204:         while time.time() - t < timeout:
205:             if not self.rd():
206:                 return False
207:             resp = self.buf[mark:]
208:             if echo is not None and echo not in resp:
209:                 continue
210:             if len(self.buf) > mark and resp.rstrip().endswith("GXemul>"):
211:                 return True
212:         return False
213: 
214:     def send(self, x, timeout=60):
215:         b = (x + "\n").encode("latin1")
216:         _mark = len(self.buf)   # #hygienecensus: `_mark`, the spelling gate 6's fresh-mark census counts
217:         n = 0
218:         try:
219:             while n < len(b):
220:                 n += os.write(self.fd, b[n:])
221:         except OSError:
222:             self.dead = True
223:             return False
224:         return self.wait(mark=_mark, echo=x if x else None, timeout=timeout)
225: 
226:     def reg(self, name, cpu=None, cr=False):

writer=re.search(r'pywrite \"\\"'$G" "'"\\"'$MODE" "'"\\"'$'"\\{GF\\[@\\]\\}\" <<\\'PY\\'\\n(.*?)\\nPY',deriver,re.S).group(1)
def run_writer(text,mode,names):
    outputs=[]
    class MemOut(io.StringIO):
        def write(self,s): outputs.append(s); return super().write(s)
    def mock_open(name,mode='r',**kw): return MemOut() if mode=='w' else io.StringIO(text)
    msg=io.StringIO()
    try:
        with patch('io.open',mock_open),patch.object(sys,'argv',['-', 'in-memory-gate',mode,*names]),contextlib.redirect_stdout(msg):
            exec(compile(writer,'deriver-grandfather-writer','exec'),{})
        return ''.join(outputs),msg.getvalue(),'ok'
    except Exception as e: return ''.join(outputs),msg.getvalue(),repr(e)

   **(d)** `WHOLE_FULL_RULING` can authorize a write accidentally through an inherited, previously exported value. The implementation accepts every nonempty string—including `"0"` and whitespace—and does not bind it to the proposed change. An invocation-specific option carrying a ruling reference, recorded alongside the pin change, would better express deliberate authorization. The present mechanism checks for an override string, not an applicable owner ruling.
